```text
1	We analyze the distribution and characteristics of binary classification errors (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) across the 8-generator LOGO evaluation ($N$\,=\,29{,}095 samples, overall error rate 28.08\%).
2	
3	\paragraph{Error asymmetry.}
4	Errors are heavily asymmetric: 86.2\% are false negatives (gen-2+ predicted as gen-1) and only 13.8\% are false positives (gen-1 predicted as gen-2+).
5	The classifier systematically under-predicts depth, consistent with the DDR pattern: shallow gen-2+ samples that have not yet accumulated sufficient distributional drift are indistinguishable from gen-1.
6	
7	\paragraph{Per-depth error rates.}
8	Error rates decrease monotonically with depth: depth-2 samples are the primary failure point (48.42\% error rate), followed by depth-3 (29.17\%) and depth-4 (20.32\%).
9	Depth-1 false positive rate is 7.11\%.
10	The monotonic decrease confirms that deeper generations accumulate progressively stronger depth signatures; the gen-1$\to$gen-2 transition is therefore the hardest to detect.
11	
12	\paragraph{Per-generator variation.}
13	Table~\ref{tab:error-by-gen} shows error rates vary substantially across generators, correlating with model scale: larger models (Mistral-7B, Qwen3-8B) produce text closer to gen-1 characteristics, which makes depth discrimination harder.
14	
15	\begin{table}[h]
16	\centering
17	\small
18	\caption{Binary classification error rates by generator (8-gen LOGO). Sorted by error rate descending.}
19	\label{tab:error-by-gen}
20	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
21	\toprule
22	\textbf{Generator} & \textbf{Family} & \textbf{Samples} & \textbf{Errors} & \textbf{Error rate} \\
23	\midrule
24	Mistral-7B-v0.3 & Mistral & 3{,}853 & 1{,}734 & 45.00\% \\
25	Qwen3-8B        & Qwen    & 3{,}502 & 1{,}197 & 34.18\% \\
26	Llama-3.2-3B    & Llama   & 3{,}685 & 1{,}112 & 30.18\% \\
27	OLMo-2-1B      & OLMo    & 3{,}718 & 1{,}054 & 28.35\% \\
28	Pythia-2.8B     & Pythia  & 3{,}614 &   866 & 23.96\% \\
29	Qwen3-1.7B     & Qwen    & 3{,}621 &   814 & 22.48\% \\
30	Qwen3-0.6B     & Qwen    & 3{,}542 &   745 & 21.03\% \\
31	Pythia-1B       & Pythia  & 3{,}560 &   648 & 18.20\% \\
32	\bottomrule
33	\end{tabular}
34	\end{table}
35	
36	\paragraph{Feature-level explanation.}
37	Mann-Whitney U tests comparing correctly vs.\ incorrectly classified samples reveal that drifting features show significant distributional differences between correct and incorrect predictions: entropy\_mean ($p < 10^{-300}$), TTR ($p < 10^{-300}$), and hapax\_ratio ($p < 10^{-300}$).
38	In contrast, shape features show no discriminating power: entropy\_max ($p = 0.584$) and tail\_decay ($p = 0.653$).
39	Misclassified gen-2+ samples have systematically higher TTR (0.464 vs.\ 0.302 for correct) and entropy\_mean (2.26 vs.\ 1.57); these samples retain gen-1-like lexical diversity and have not undergone the distributional convergence characteristic of deeper generations.
40	This is consistent with the DDR pattern (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}): errors concentrate where depth-accumulated drift is insufficient, and shape features (which are near-stationary by design) provide no additional discriminative signal for error correction.
41	
```

### Assistant

_source line 178 · 2026-05-19T11:05:45.499Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a thorough understanding of the paper. Let me compile my detailed review, focusing on method rigor and experimental design as directed.

Key observations:

**Strengths:**
1. Well-motivated formulation of generation depth classification beyond binary detection
2. Extensive ablation and control experiments (LOGO, LOFO, seed control, temperature, cross-model, cross-domain, length-matched, etc.)
3. DDR tri-partition is supported by multiple converging metrics (ρ, d, Glass's Δ, Cliff's δ, TOST, holdout splits)
4. Transparent about limitations (instruction tuning, scale, temperature sensitivity)


5. Rigorous statistical approach with Bonferroni correction, bootstrap confidence intervals, and TOST equivalence testing, plus strong transfer performance across 56 generator pairs

**Weaknesses to flag:**

1. [Critical] The 3/1/3 partition relies on post-hoc domain-informed refinement despite the data-driven framing—clustering alone favors k=2 (silhouette=0.632), and the final three-way split uses binary-task Cohen's d thresholds for entropy minimization to override what the unsupervised analysis suggests. The paper acknowledges this somewhat transparently in the appendix, but the gap between the clustering evidence and the chosen partition warrants clearer discussion of this methodological choice.

2. [Major] The generator identity confound in cross-model chains isn't fully resolved by the transfer analysis, since those are different experimental setups—transfer trains on one generator and tests on another, while cross-model chains intermix generators within each chain, potentially allowing the classifier to exploit model-specific distributional signatures at different depths rather than genuine depth effects. The paper recognizes this risk but doesn't empirically rule it out.

3. [Major] The temperature ablation is too narrow—only three points (0.5, 0.7, 0.9) tested, showing severe degradation at t=0.9 (-9.3 to -26.0 percentage points), yet the claimed reliable range of t ∈ [0.5, 0.7] is restrictive for real-world use where temperatures vary widely. Critically, t=1.0 is missing from the main ablation, appearing only in a confounded cross-domain experiment.

4. [Major] Seed sharing across all generators in the evaluation introduces systematic bias that could artificially inflate performance metrics.

5. [Major] The experimental design uses inconsistent generator subsets (ranging from 1 to 8 generators across different experiments), which makes it difficult to compare results across conditions and raises questions about whether reported improvements like the QLoRA result (F1=0.905) are truly comparable.

6. [Major] The baseline comparison has a fairness issue: the 4-detector ensemble actually achieves comparable performance (F1=0.708) to the 7-feature set (F1=0.705) while using fewer features, which somewhat undermines the claimed advantages of the 7-feature approach beyond interpretability.

7. [Minor] The scope of multiple testing correction using Bonferroni is applied within families of tests, but many other comparisons throughout the paper—across temperature conditions, generator pairs, and domains—aren't explicitly corrected.

8. [Minor] The location/shape decomposition interpretation lacks formal validation; it's presented as consistent with the data but remains post-hoc, which the paper itself acknowledges.

9. [Minor] The downstream utility experiment uses only 3 seeds per condition, making the conclusion about data quantity dominating depth composition somewhat overstated given the limited statistical power.

10. [Minor] The rationale for selecting the seven features isn't fully detailed...

The feature selection process wasn't systematic—the paper chose features a priori from two complementary perspectives without exploring the broader feature space. Including syntactic and semantic features might substantially shift how the DDR is characterized.

I'm also thinking through some specific questions for the authors: whether testing temperature values at 1.0 and 1.2 would reveal important gaps in the ablation (especially since 1.0 is common in practice), how the cross-model chain experiment's sample-level CV approach prevents the classifier from picking up generator-specific patterns rather than true depth signals, and what the actual class balance looks like after filtering and collapse across the 37,058 samples.

There's also the question of whether the seed-sharing control in the independent-seed experiment generalizes beyond the two generators tested, and a puzzling discrepancy with entropy min where Cliff's δ suggests a medium effect but Cohen's d is nearly zero—the paper's explanation about distributional asymmetry doesn't fully resolve this tension.

The references look solid overall, with good coverage of concurrent work on model degradation, Markovian chains, and detection baselines.

Now let me double-check some of the reported numbers. Looking at Table 1's LOGO results, the average binary F1 across the eight generators comes to about 0.695, which matches the reported 0.694 within rounding error. But I'm noticing a discrepancy between the baselines table and Table 1 where the 7-feature method shows different F1 values, and the caption seems to cut off before explaining this.

The difference appears to stem from how the results are aggregated—Table 2 pools predictions across all generators before computing F1, while Table 1 averages the per-generator F1 scores. That explains the 0.705 versus 0.694 gap, though it's a bit confusing as presented. More importantly, looking at the actual baseline comparisons in Table 2, the detection-based features are actually performing competitively or even slightly better than the 7-feature ensemble, which contradicts what seems to be claimed about the ensemble's superiority.

The 7-feature method does beat individual baselines on 3-class F1, but the 4-detector ensemble with surface features matches or exceeds it on both binary and 3-class metrics. This is a meaningful finding that suggests repurposed detection scores can be just as effective as depth-specialized features, but it's somewhat downplayed in the paper's framing. I'm also noticing the paper relies on multiple statistical tests, which raises some concerns about multiple comparisons.

However, the authors do address this reasonably well. They apply Bonferroni correction within test families, and the robustness checks show the 3/1/3 partition holds across different threshold ranges. The holdout validation across generator subsets also supports the stability of their partition scheme, so while the threshold choices weren't pre-registered, the methodology seems sound enough.

Now I'm thinking through the LOGO evaluation more carefully. The key issue is that when you hold out one generator, the training set still contains gen-0 texts from the other seven generators—but these are the same seeds as the held-out generator's gen-0. The paper acknowledges this and uses binary F1 excluding gen-0 as the main metric, which is the right call. For gen-1, there's a potential confound where all generators' continuations stem from the same seed texts, just with different continuations. But their independent-seed experiment largely resolves this concern, showing F1 scores of 0.680 versus 0.694, which suggests the seed content isn't driving the results.

Now I'm ready to write up a thorough review. But first, let me dig into the entropy min Cliff's δ issue more carefully. The paper reports Cliff's δ = 0.354 (medium effect) for entropy min's gen-1 versus gen-2+ distributions, while Cohen's d is only 0.016. The footnote explains this as a distributional shape asymmetry that rank-based statistics pick up but location measures miss. But if Cliff's δ is medium, that means there's a real ordinal difference between the distributions—it's just not reflected in the means. That's actually interesting and raises a question about whether entropy min shows meaningful depth differences.

If entropy min has medium Cliff's δ, does that really support calling it "near-stationary"? The paper's classification relies on Cohen's d, which is consistent with their location-versus-shape framing, but a reviewer could push back and argue that any feature with medium Cliff's δ shouldn't get that label. The paper acknowledges this boundary status, but I think there's a genuine tension here worth flagging.

Looking at the overall assessment now: the paper has real strengths—it tackles an interesting and timely question, runs extensive experiments with solid controls, is transparent about limitations, and uncovers an intriguing empirical finding with DDR. The writing is clear and the methodology mostly holds up. But the weaknesses are also significant: limited practical utility since it only covers base models in a narrow temperature range at a specific scale, moderate classification performance with binary F1 around 0.694, and the depth distinction itself has some fuzzy boundaries.

The DDR finding is interesting but mostly descriptive—it documents the phenomenon without explaining the underlying mechanism. The 3/1/3 partition involves interpretive choices that aren't fully grounded in the data. Weighing everything, this feels like a solid empirical contribution with a novel task formulation, but the practical constraints and lack of mechanistic insight limit how much impact it can have. I'm settling on a Findings-level rating around 3.0 to 3.5, with soundness at 3.5 due to good controls despite some post-hoc aspects, excitement at 3.0 given the interesting formulation but limited applicability, and an overall score of 3.0 to 3.5.

Actually, stepping back—the experimental work is quite thorough with extensive ablations and an honest limitations section. The depth classification formulation is genuinely novel, and the DDR itself is a real finding. But the severe practical constraints on base models only, the moderate classification performance, and the post-hoc nature of the partition interpretation are real weaknesses. I'm going to finalize this at Overall 3.0 (Findings), Soundness 3.5, Excitement 2.5, with high confidence at 4/5. Looking at the scoring rubric more carefully, a 3.0 (Findings level) feels right—the paper presents solid empirical work characterizing an interesting phenomenon, but it's limited to base models in a narrow setting without broader practical applications or deeper theoretical insights.
```

group。3/1/3 的关键步骤——用 binary-task Cohen's d 将 entropy min 归入 near-stationary——是 domain-informed 的解释性判断，不是数据驱动的发现。\n\n更重要的是，entropy min 的 Cliff's δ = 0.354（\"medium\" per Romano et al. 2006）与 Cohen's d = 0.016 严重不一致（Table in Appendix V, Table on effect sizes）。这意味着 entropy min 在 gen-1 vs gen-2+ 之间存在可被 rank-based 方法检测的有意义差异，只是不体现在均值上。用 Cohen's d（位置度量）而非 Cliff's δ（序数度量）来判定 near-stationarity，在 ordinal classification 论文中是一个不对称的方法论选择，恰好支持了作者想要的 location/shape 叙事。\n\n**建议**: 将 Contribution 2 的 framing 调整为 \"data-driven primary split (k=2) + domain-informed refinement (3/1/3)\"，并在正文中讨论 entropy min 的 6-gen LOGO（vs 主结果 8-gen）；XGBoost baseline 也是 6-gen (F1=0.789 vs 8-gen F1=0.762)。直接比较 0.905 vs 0.762 高估了 QLoRA 增量。\n- Temperature ablation 混合了 6-gen LOGO（Pythia-2.8B, Qwen3-1.7B）和 8-gen LOGO（OLMo, Qwen3-0.6B），如 Appendix H 脚注所述。\n- Prompt-type 分析仅用 Pythia-2.8B 一个 generator。\n- Instruct 实验用 3 个不同 generator 的 within-model 5-fold CV，而非 LOGO。\n\n**建议**: 在正文中添加一段明确说明 generator subset 不一致及其对比较的影响；QLoRA 增量应对比同 subset 的 XGBoost baseline (0.905 vs 0.789 = +11.6 pp，而非暗示与 0.762 对比)。实际上论文在 Appendix E 中已经给出了正确的 baseline 但主结果段 (§4.1) 引用 \"+11.6 pp\" 时未指明这是 6-gen 对比。\n\n**W3 [Major] Temperature 消融覆盖不足，可靠工作范围过窄。** 仅测试 t ∈ {0.5, 0.7, 0.9} 三个点，且仅覆盖 4 个 generator（3 families）。t=0.9 已导致 -9.3 to -26.0 pp 降级，但 t=1.0 在主消融中缺失（仅在 cross-domain 实验中以 confounded 方式出现）。方法的 \"可靠工作范围\" t ∈ [0.5, 0.7] 对实际部署而言过于狭窄——很多 LLM 部署使用 t=0.8-1.0。\n\n此外，cross-temperature transfer 实验（Appendix O）仅替换一个 generator (Qwen3-1.7B) 的温度，其他 5 个保持 t=0.7。这不能充分代表全局温度 mismatch 场景。\n\n**建议**: 至少补充 t=0.8 和 t=1.0 的消融（即使只在部分 generator 上），并在 Discussion 中更直接地讨论这一限制对实用性的影响。\n\n**W4 [Major] Cross-model chain confound 未被 transfer analysis 充分排除。** Cross-model chain F1=0.764 高于 LOGO F1=0.694。论文承认 \"generator identity information in mixed chains could partially confound the depth signal\"（§4.1），并引用 transfer analysis (mean F1=0.810) 作为反驳。\n\n但 transfer analysis 的设计与 cross-model chains 不同：前者 train-on-A/test-on-B 是纯 single-generator 评估，后者是 mixed-generator chains。在 mixed chains 中，如果 depth-1 由 model-A 生成而 depth-2 由 model-B 生成，classifier 可能通过 model identity 而非 depth signature 区分它们。Transfer analysis 无法排除这一特定 confound。\n\n**建议**: 要么设计一个控制实验（如对 cross-model chains 使用 single-generator control chains 共享相同 seed），要么在正文中明确承认 transfer analysis 不能完全排除此 confound（当前 \"rules out this confound\" 的表述过强）。\n\n**W5 [Major] Baseline 对比的 framing 需要更坦诚。** Table 2 显示 4-detector+surface ensemble（6 features）在 binary F1 (0.708) 和 3-class F1 (0.795) 上均超过 7-feature ensemble (0.705, 0.774)。4-detector ensemble（4 features）在 binary F1 (0.700 vs 0.705) 上几乎持平。论文用 \"contextualize depth discrimination ability rather than claim competitive advantage\"（§4.3）来 frame 这一比较，但正文声称 \"The 7-feature ensemble exceeds all individual baselines in 3-class F1\"——这虽然字面上对（individual baselines），但忽略了 ensemble baselines 已经超越 7-feature 的事实。\n\n这对论文的价值判断有重要影响：如果 repurposed detection scores 的 ensemble 已经达到甚至超过 depth-specialized features 的分类性能，那么 DDR 分析的增量贡献更多在于 interpretability 和 mechanistic insight，而非分类性能。论文应该更清晰地传达这一点。\n\n**W6 [Minor] Independent-seed 控制仅覆盖 2 个 generator。** Appendix R 的 independent-seed 实验仅用 Pythia-2.8B 和 OLMo-2-1B。虽然 F1 差距小（0.680 vs 0.694），但无法确认此控制对全部 8 个 generator 成立，特别是 Mistral-7B（LOGO F1 最低的 generator）。\n\n**W7 [Minor] Downstream utility 实验统计效力不足。** n=3 seeds per condition，TOST 结论的 generalizability 受限（论文在 Appendix L 中有承认）。但 §5 引用此结果时仍作了较强表述（\"does not detect a depth-composition effect\"）。在 n=3 的情况下，absence of evidence ≠ evidence of absence。\n\n**W8 [Minor] Table 1 与 Table 2 报告不同聚合方式的 F1 造成混淆。** Table 1 报告 per-generator macro average binary F1=0.694；Table 2 报告 pooled binary F1=0.705。同一方法在两个表中差 1.1 pp。虽然 caption 有解释（\"pooled evaluation; slightly higher than per-generator macro average\"），但读者容易混淆。建议在 Table 2 中同时报告两种聚合方式，或至少在正文中明确提示。\n\n---\n\n## Questions (需要作者回答)\n\n**Q1.** Cross-model chains 使用 sample-level 5-fold CV 而非 generator-level splits。由于不同 generator 可能贡献 chain 中不同深度的文本，classifier 是否可能学到 generator-specific features 而非 depth features？能否提供一个 per-chain split（同一 chain 的所有 depths 在同一 fold）的结果？\n\n**Q2.** 37,058 samples 在 gen-0 / gen-1 / gen-2+ 三个类别上的实际分布是什么？gen-2+ 合并了 depth 2-4，因此 gen-2+ 的样本量约为 gen-1 的 3 倍。这种 class imbalance 是否影响了 F1 报告？\n\n**Q3.** Entropy min 的 Cliff's δ = 0.354 (medium) 与 Cohen's d = 0.016 (near-zero) 之间的巨大差异意味着什么？如果 near-stationary 的定义基于 Cohen's d 但 Cliff's δ 显示 medium effect，这是否意味着 \"near-stationary\" 这个标签对 entropy min 不准确？\n\n**Q4.** Cross-domain 实验使用了 t=1.0/top-p=1.0（而非 t=0.7/p=0.9），与 main experiment 参数不一致。如果在 t=0.7 下 Academic domain binary F1 仅为 0.628（Appendix I），这是否意味着 DDR 的 domain generalization 在方法的 \"可靠工作范围\" 内其实较弱？\n\n**Q5.** OLMo temperature 实验使用了 OLMo-2-0425-1B（April 2025 release）而非原始 OLMo-2-1B-hf。这两个 model 的差异是否可能影响 temperature sensitivity 结论？\n\n---\n\n## Missing References\n\n无明显遗漏。覆盖了 detection baselines（DetectGPT, FastDetectGPT, Binoculars, LD-Score）、model collapse（Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al.）、concurrent work（Geng et al., Mohamadi et al., Wang et al.）。可考虑补充：\n- Watermark decay 相关工作（已引用 Kirchenbauer et al. 和 chainwash）\n- 若存在 2025-2026 年的 multi-depth detection 新工作，应确认是否需要引用\n\n---\n\n## Overall Assessment\n\n**Overall**: 3.0 (Findings)\n\n**一句话总结**: 提出了有意义的新任务形式化（depth classification）和有趣的经验发现（DDR tri-partition），但实用性受限于 base model + 窄温度范围 + 中等分类性能，且 DDR 的解释性贡献大于方法论贡献。\n\n## 官方维度打分（ARR review form）\n\n| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |\n|------|------|----------|\n| Soundness | 3.5/5 | 统计检验严谨，控制实验全面；但 3/1/3 partition 有 post-hoc 成分，cross-model confound 未完全排除，generator subset 不一致影响可比性 |\n| Excitement | 标准。 |\n\n---\n\n## Confidence: 4/5\n\n熟悉 AI-generated text detection 和统计检验方法论；对 model collapse 文献有中等了解。对本文的统计方法评估有信心，对实际部署需求的判断基于领域一般知识。",

[Round 27 审稿请求]:
## 交叉分享 — Reviewer 2 的完整审稿意见

请逐条回应 Reviewer 2 的每个 Weakness 和 Question：用 **[AGREE]** 表示同意并说明理由，或 **[REBUT]** 表示反驳并提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）。

---

以下是 Reviewer 2 的完整审稿原文：

### Summary
本文提出 generation depth classification 任务（gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+ 三分类），在 8 个 base model 生成链上发现 differential depth response (DDR) 三分区结构（drifting/intermediate/near-stationary），并用 7 特征集合在 LOGO 评估下达到 binary F1=0.694。贡献以经验性分析为主，统计分析细致，但 base-model-only 限制和中等分类精度约束了实际影响力。

### Strengths
S1. 任务定义清晰且有意义。
S2. 统计分析极其严谨。
S3. 评估设计周全、对自身局限诚实。
S4. 与 concurrent work 的 positioning 准确。
S5. 写作质量高，无明显 AI 写作标志。
S6. 附录实验丰富。

### Weaknesses

**N1 [Major] Base-model-only 限制严重削弱实际影响力。**
- Introduction 以 data provenance 作核心动机，但 web 文本主要由 instruct model 生成
- Instruct DDR 退化（silhouette 0.632→0.338），跨条件迁移接近 chance
- Motivation 与 applicability 的 disconnect 削弱 excitement
- QLoRA on instruct 已达 0.89-0.91，可在正文更显著提及以缓解此问题

**N2 [Major] DDR 新颖性需要更精确的 calibration。**
- Location shift + shape stability 在 temperature-controlled sampling 下可能是可预期的
- Discussion §5 承认只是 "informal conjecture"
- DDR 更多是已知 distributional drift 的 feature-level 描述而非新现象
- 适合 Findings 但 Main 门槛更高

**W1 [Major] 分类精度中等（binary F1=0.694），实用价值存疑。**
- ~30% 错误率，gen-1 vs gen-2+ 区分能力有限
- QLoRA 0.905 是 within-generator 结果，实际场景 generator identity 未知

**W2 [Minor] Generator subset 跨实验不一致。**
- 5/6/8-gen 三种子集，跨实验比较困难
- Instruct 实验 baseline 与 Table 1 不直接可比

**W3 [Minor] Abstract 偏短（~153 words），以负面限制结尾弱化 pitch。**

**W4 [Minor] 补充材料 anonymous_supplement.tgz 文件缺失。**

### Questions
Q1. DDR location/shape decomposition 是否可从 temperature-controlled sampling 理论直接推导？
Q2. QLoRA on instruct (F1 0.89-0.91) 为什么不在正文更突出讨论？
Q3. Transfer analysis (F1=0.810) 是否完全排除 cross-model chain 的 generator identity confound？
Q4. TTR-hapax 保留两者的 marginal LOFO gains 具体是多少？若 <1pp 是否值得？
Q5. Mistral-7B binary F1=0.633 显著偏低的原因？

### Overall: 3.0 (Findings), Confidence 4/5

---

请对每个 N1, N2, W1-W4 和 Q1-Q5 逐条给出 [AGREE] 或 [REBUT] 回应。

_source line 191 · 2026-05-19T11:08:55.691Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me carefully go through Reviewer 2's points and respond to each one based on my thorough reading of the paper. I need to decide for each whether I agree or rebut, citing specific evidence from the paper.

Let me go through each:

**N1 [Major] Base-model-only limitation:**
I largely agree with this. The paper's motivation is data provenance but base models aren't what produces most web text. The instruct experiments show severe degradation. However, I should note that the paper IS transparent about this in Limitations. But the motivation-applicability disconnect is real.
 
**N2 [Major] DDR novelty needs calibration:**
The location/shape decomposition under fixed temperature is somewhat predictable from sampling theory, which is a fair point. But the specific tri-partition and how it's leveraged for depth classification appears novel—I haven't seen this reported elsewhere. So while the reviewer has a point about the theoretical foundations, the paper does contribute something beyond just characterizing known drift patterns.

**W1 [Major] Classification precision is moderate:**
The binary F1 of 0.694 does represent a meaningful error rate around 30%. I need to verify the experimental setup more carefully—the QLoRA results appear to use a 6-generator leave-one-out approach rather than standard cross-validation, and the appendix details seem to cut off mid-explanation.

**W2 [Minor] Generator subset inconsistency:**
This is a valid concern that I flagged in my own review as well.

**W3 [Minor] Abstract too short, ends negatively:**
The abstract runs about 153 words and concludes with a limitation statement about depth signal degradation under instruction tuning. Ending on a negative note is unconventional for an abstract, though the concern itself is legitimate.

**W4 [Minor] Supplement file missing:**
I can't directly verify whether the referenced tgz file is actually present, but if it's missing that's a fair issue to raise.

**Q1:** The location/shape decomposition question is reasonable. Temperature and nucleus sampling parameters do constrain softmax shape, so some stability is expected, but the specific quantitative breakdown of feature drift is novel and worth examining.

**Q2:** The QLoRA results on instruction-tuned models deserve more prominence—they're currently tucked away in the discussion and appendix rather than featured in the main narrative.

**Q3:** This transfer analysis concern mirrors a point I made in my own review. The cross-model confound isn't fully resolved by the current transfer analysis approach.

**Q4:**
Looking at the LOFO results, dropping hapax costs -0.42pp and dropping TTR costs -0.55pp—individually modest gains, yet the paper keeps both features. With r=0.97 correlation between them, this seems like a legitimate question about whether the marginal contribution justifies retaining both.

**Q5:** The Mistral-7B F1 gap to 0.633 is partially explained by scale effects and reference affinity, but the story feels incomplete. Mistral-7B is the largest non-Qwen model tested, and the Pythia-410M reference might have weaker architectural alignment with it. The cross-reference spread of only 0.013 suggests reference affinity alone doesn't account for the full performance drop.
```

</details>
